3	# Build backend: hatchling. `backend/` is an intentional NAMESPACE package

10	# which PEP 621 cannot express. Install them separately — see [project.optional-dependencies]
11	# `gpu` (documentation only) and the README. CI installs CPU torch on its own line.
12	[build-system]
13	requires = ["hatchling"]
14	build-backend = "hatchling.build"

16	[project]

--- EXECUTIVE SUMMARY ---
 Ship the engine as ONE pip-installable artifact whose behavior is selected entirely by the already-merged decoupled-compute seam (deployment.profile / compute_target / StorageBackend / ComputeBackend), fronted by a thin friendly launcher and a Windows installer — NOT a frozen monolith and NOT an Electron/Tauri shell. Distribution leads with a generated install script over uv (auto-installs Python + the right torch index) plus an Inno Setup Windows installer; a Docker image (the existing deploy/cloudrun/Dockerfile, micromamba-ified) serves the cloud/server profile and the BYO-GPU path. All cloud, edge-auth, cost-ledger, and training features stay default-OFF so the local single-user app is byte-identical to the verified CUJs.

The app defaults to the truly-local preset (skip_ai_handoff handled per tier) so the out-of-box experience is zero-cloud unless the user opts in.

▸ Primary distribution channel
  options: ['PyPI wheel + pip', 'pipx', 'uv tool install + generated script', 'Inno Setup installer', 'PyInstaller frozen onefile']
  REC: Inno Setup installer (non-tech) backed by a uv-based generated script (power/cross-platform); reject PyInstaller

  options: ['Both torches in one venv (impossible)', 'conda-pack relocatable tarball', 'micromamba+conda-forge env on first run', 'ONNX export (no torch on WASB path)', 'Docker-only']
  REC: micromamba+conda-forge now as the bridge; ONNX export as the strategic fix (owner-gated re-validation)
  why: Two incompatible ABIs cannot share a venv or a freeze. micromamba is BSD and sidesteps the Anaconda-defaults ToS the current Dockerfile:35 still trips. ONNX (onnxruntime MIT) dissolves the dual-env, ships tens-of-MB not 5GB, and gives CPU+CUDA+DirectML+CoreML — but needs a byte-parity gate on a real GPU (owner-side, offline CI can't prove numbers).

An LGPL bundle would force swapping libx264 -> openh264/NVENC at BOTH sites (compiler.py:253, video.py:42).

Get owner re-ratification in writing.

### 57. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.437Z)

Done. I synced, ran an exhaustive 29-agent research workflow, **re-verified every load-bearing claim at `file:line` myself**, and wrote it up as a tracked project doc: **[docs/PACKAGING_PLAN.md](docs/PACKAGING_PLAN.md)** (untracked/uncommitted — I won't open a PR unless you ask). Here's the plan.

## The core idea
**One pip-installable artifact whose behaviour is selected entirely by the decoupled-compute seam you already merged** (`deployment.profile` / `compute_target` / Storage+Compute backends), fronted by a friendly launcher + a Windows installer. **Not** a frozen monolith, **not** an Electron/Tauri shell. Local-vs-cloud is genuinely *one config flip* — you built that seam (M1–M8); this is the **distribution layer** on top, not a rewrite.

## The constraint that forces the shape
Two facts are load-bearing and verified:
1. **torch is deliberately out of `pyproject`** (CUDA/CPU wheels need `--index-url`, which PEP 621 can't express) — `pip install .` gives a torch-less install.
2. **Native WASB needs a *second, incompatible* torch env** (conda py3.8 + torch 1.11+cu113) shelled out via `$WASB_PYTHON` (`native_wasb_runner.py:89`), while the main engine is torch 2.6.

→ **No single venv or freeze can deliver the GPU-quality path.** So the honest v1 is a **LIGHT tier** (Gemini + motion + cpu-mock, *zero* torch/GPU) that preserves CUJ-1..5/8 and runs on any CPU box. Native-WASB (CUJ-6/7) ships **owner-only** until two blockers clear.

## Three tiers
| Tier | Who | Contents | Install |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LIGHT** (v1 default) | Non-tech coach on Windows; any CPU | wheel + bundled SPA + Gemini/motion/cpu-mock; no torch/weights; ffmpeg via winget-offer | **Inno Setup .exe** → launcher → auto-opens `localhost:8000`; uv script for power users |
| **HEAVY/local-GPU** | Owner + opt-in power user | + micromamba WASB env via `$WASB_PYTHON` + SHA-256-pinned weights | opt-in "Enable GPU" step; falls back to LIGHT |
| **CLOUD/server** | GCP / BYO-GPU-VM | the `deploy/cloudrun` image (micromamba-ified) behind Cloudflare Access | `gcloud run jobs create` / VM scripts |

## How the three hard problems get solved
- **Dual-torch:** v1 carries *no* torch. HEAVY uses a separate **micromamba+conda-forge** env (BSD — also fixes the **Anaconda-ToS** trap in `Dockerfile:33-37`). **Strategic fix: export WASB to ONNX** — `onnxruntime` (MIT, tens of MB, CPU+CUDA+DirectML) *dissolves the dual-env entirely* and the `DetectorRunner` ABC already names an `ONNXRunner`.
- **Weights:** never bake them in; fetch on first run, SHA-256-pinned + resumable. **Public download must NOT auto-fetch until counsel clears WASB-C3** (`gate_verdict` already hardcodes `ship=False`).
- **ffmpeg:** don't redistribute (the encoder is GPL `libx264`); one resolver helper + a `winget install Gyan.FFmpeg` first-run offer.
- **SPA-in-another-repo:** build `khelsutra/web` at package time, vendor `web/dist/` into the wheel (hatchling force-include) served single-origin. The SPA already uses same-origin `BASE='/api'` → **zero SPA code change**, and it collapses the appliance reverse-proxy into one FastAPI process.

## Phased plan (small, default-OFF, reversible PRs)
- **P0 — engine prerequisites (Claude-doable, no behaviour change):** ① **app-data HOME** threaded through config/`.env`/DB/output/static (today all CWD-relative, and `doctor.py` writes `config.json` to a *different* place than the server reads — a silent data-loss footgun); ② **redact `/api/config`** (returns the whole `AppConfig` unauthenticated today); ③ config-driven bind/CORS; ④ declare **scipy** (undeclared serving dep) + license-scan + built-artifact CI lanes; ⑤ single-instance lock.
- **P1 — UI bundling + the 3 net-new endpoints** (`/api/capabilities`, `/api/videos` list, `/api/reels`) the wizard needs (confirmed absent) + `/api/version` contract test.
- **P2 — LIGHT installer + first-run wizard** (key/compute/videos, en/kn/hi) + DB backup-before-migrate + uninstall manifest.
- **P3 — HEAVY/cloud (largely owner-gated):** micromamba, weight fetcher (C3-gated), "push to cloud" toggle (confirm + INR estimate), M9 edge-auth.
- **P4 — ONNX export (dissolves dual-env) + training/annotation decisions** (recommend deferring training-in-UI; recommend an in-browser annotator over bundling VLC).

**Every completed CUJ is mapped** in §9 of the doc — CUJ-6/7 (native WASB) is the *one* deliberately absent from the LIGHT default, flagged explicitly rather than silently dropped (the honest version of "preserve every CUJ").

## The decisions I need from you (load-bearing, before P2)
The biggest two — I'd value a quick answer since they gate everything downstream:

33	def _deep_merge(base: dict[str, Any], override: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:

47	def _resolve_explicit_profile(file_data: dict[str, Any]) -> str:

80	    user's intent is lost — collect every such key so load_config can warn.
81	    """
82	    unknown: list[str] = []
83	    fields = model_cls.model_fields
84	    for key, val in data.items():
85	        if key not in fields:
86	            unknown.append(prefix + key)
87	            continue
88	        ann = fields[key].annotation
89	        if isinstance(val, dict) and isinstance(ann, type) and issubclass(ann, BaseModel):
90	            unknown.extend(_unknown_key_paths(val, ann, f"{prefix}{key}."))
91	    return unknown

2	import os
3	from typing import Any, Optional
4	
5	
6	def output_base(config: Any) -> str:
7	    """The configured base dir for a segmenter's LOCAL scratch/staging artifacts
8	    (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M3). Returns ``output.output_dir`` (default ``"output"``) so the
9	    chunk-staging / AI-handoff / temp dirs follow ``--output-dir`` / ``output.output_dir`` instead of
10	    a hardcoded ``./output``. The DEFAULT is ``"output"`` → byte-identical to the prior literal.
11	
12	    This is the LOCAL output dir, deliberately NOT the storage ``workspace_root`` (which may be a
13	    ``gs://`` URI you can't ``makedirs``). Accepts the typed AppConfig or the legacy dict-shim
14	    (``.get("output", {}).get("output_dir")``); any miss or empty value falls back to ``"output"``."""
15	    try:
16	        out = config.get("output", {}).get("output_dir")
17	    except Exception:
18	        out = None
19	    return out or "output"
20	
21	
22	def safe_output_filename(name: str) -> str:
23	    """Validate an output filename is a BARE name (no dir part, no traversal, not absolute).
24	
25	    `os.path.join(output_dir, name)` silently discards `output_dir` when `name` is absolute,
26	    and `..` segments escape it — so an unchecked `output_filename` is an arbitrary-write
27	    primitive. Returns the name unchanged when safe; raises ValueError otherwise.
28	    """
29	    if not name or not name.strip():
30	        raise ValueError("output_filename must not be empty")
31	    if "\x00" in name:
32	        raise ValueError("output_filename must not contain a null byte")
33	    if "/" in name or "\\" in name:
34	        raise ValueError(f"output_filename must not contain a path separator: {name!r}")
35	    if os.path.isabs(name) or name != os.path.basename(name) or name in (".", ".."):
36	        raise ValueError(f"output_filename must be a bare filename, got: {name!r}")
37	    return name
38	
39	
40	def resolve_under_root(path: str, root: str) -> str:
41	    """Return the real path, asserting it is contained under `root` (raises ValueError if not)."""
42	    rp = os.path.realpath(path)
43	    rr = os.path.realpath(root)
44	    try:
45	        contained = os.path.commonpath([rp, rr]) == rr
46	    except ValueError:  # different drives on Windows
47	        contained = False
48	    if not contained:
49	        raise ValueError(f"path {path!r} escapes the allowed root {root!r}")
50	    return rp
51	
52	
53	def validate_input_path(path: str, allowed_root: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
54	    """Reject null bytes and (when an allowed_root is configured) out-of-root inputs.
55	
56	    With no allowed_root (default, single-user local mode) only the null-byte check applies,
57	    so the local 'point it at any of my videos' workflow is preserved. Setting an
58	    allowed_root (a future hosted/tenant deployment) enforces containment.
59	    """
60	    if "\x00" in path:
61	        raise ValueError("video_path must not contain a null byte")
62	    if allowed_root:
63	        resolve_under_root(path, allowed_root)
64

55	    return origins or ["*"]

import os, tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from backend.utils.app_home import resolve_output_dir, default_config_path, app_home
from backend.config import save_default_config
from backend.infrastructure.database import Database

def init_like_server(output_dir_arg="output"):
    """Mirror backend/main.py:656-660 exactly: resolve output dir -> makedirs -> DB path."""
    out = resolve_output_dir(output_dir_arg)
    os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
    db_path = os.path.join(out, "sports_indexer.db")
    Database(db_path)  # creates + migrates the SQLite file
    return out, db_path

print("=== CASE 1: RALLY_APP_HOME UNSET (today's behaviour — CWD-relative) ===")
os.environ.pop("RALLY_APP_HOME", None)
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as cwd:
    os.chdir(cwd)
    print("  app_home()        :", app_home())
    print("  config path       :", default_config_path())
    out, db = init_like_server()
    print("  output dir        :", out, "(relative -> under CWD)")
    print("  DB created under CWD?:", Path(db).resolve().is_relative_to(Path(cwd).resolve()), "->", Path(db).resolve())

print()
print("=== CASE 2: RALLY_APP_HOME SET (packaged app — state roots in the app home) ===")
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as home, tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as cwd:
    os.chdir(cwd)                      # launched from a DIFFERENT dir (e.g. OS app menu)
    os.environ["RALLY_APP_HOME"] = home
    print("  RALLY_APP_HOME    :", home)
    print("  launched from CWD :", cwd)
    cfg = save_default_config()        # --setup / doctor writes here
    out, db = init_like_server()
    print("  config written to :", cfg, "| exists:", cfg.exists())
    print("  DB created under HOME?:", Path(db).resolve().is_relative_to(Path(home).resolve()), "->", Path(db).resolve())
    print("  NOTHING leaked into CWD?:", os.listdir(cwd) == [], "(cwd contents:", os.listdir(cwd), ")")
os.environ.pop("RALLY_APP_HOME", None)
print("\nPROOF OK: state follows RALLY_APP_HOME; with it unset, behaviour is byte-identical (CWD).")
PY

1	"""Regression guard for the `python -m backend.main` double-module job-hang.
2	
3	`python -m backend.main` runs main.py as `__main__`, so `main()` configures `__main__`'s globals
4	(`db_instance`/`global_config`). But `job_worker`/`uploads` resolve shared state via
5	`import backend.main` — a SEPARATE module object whose `db_instance` stays `None`. The async-job
6	drain then crashes silently on `None.claim_due_job()` and every submitted job hangs in `queued`
7	forever. The in-process tests (TestClient + monkeypatched `db_instance` + an inline executor)
8	cannot see this — only the real subprocess does. This spawns the DOCUMENTED invocation and asserts
9	a submitted job actually LEAVES `queued`.
10	"""
11	import json
12	import os
13	import socket
14	import subprocess
15	import sys
16	import time
17	import urllib.error
18	import urllib.request
19	
20	import pytest
21	
22	REPO_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
23	
24	
25	def _free_port() -> int:
26	    s = socket.socket()
27	    s.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
28	    port = int(s.getsockname()[1])
29	    s.close()
30	    return port
31	
32	
33	def _get(url: str, timeout: float = 5):
34	    with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=timeout) as r:
35	        return json.loads(r.read().decode())
36	
37	
38	def _post(url: str, payload: dict, timeout: float = 15):
39	    req = urllib.request.Request(
40	        url, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers={"content-type": "application/json"})
41	    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as r:
42	        return json.loads(r.read().decode())
43	
44	
45	def test_python_m_backend_main_drains_submitted_jobs(tmp_path):
46	    """Spawning the engine the documented way (`python -m backend.main --server`) must actually
47	    DRAIN a submitted job — it must leave `queued`, never hang. (A dummy file fails fast; the
48	    verdict is irrelevant — what matters is that the worker claimed and ran it.)"""
49	    port = _free_port()
50	    out_dir = tmp_path / "out"
51	    video = tmp_path / "dummy.mp4"
52	    video.write_bytes(b"not-a-real-video")  # exists -> the drain claims it; processing fails fast
53	    log = open(tmp_path / "server.log", "wb")
54	
55	    proc = subprocess.Popen(
56	        [sys.executable, "-m", "backend.main", "--server", "--port", str(port),
57	         "--output-dir", str(out_dir)],
58	        cwd=REPO_ROOT, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
59	    )
60	    base = f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}"
61	    try:
62	        deadline = time.time() + 90  # cold import of torch/cv2 can be slow on CI
63	        while time.time() < deadline:
64	            if proc.poll() is not None:
65	                pytest.fail(f"server exited early (code {proc.returncode}); see {tmp_path/'server.log'}")
66	            try:
67	                if _get(f"{base}/api/health").get("status") == "ok":
68	                    break
69	            except (urllib.error.URLError, ConnectionError, OSError):
70	                time.sleep(0.5)
71	        else:
72	            pytest.fail("server never became healthy")
73	
74	        job_id = _post(f"{base}/api/process",
75	                       {"video_path": str(video), "segmenter": "motion"})["job_id"]
76	
77	        status = "queued"
78	        deadline = time.time() + 45
79	        while time.time() < deadline:
80	            status = _get(f"{base}/api/jobs/{job_id}")["status"]
81	            if status != "queued":
82	                break
83	            time.sleep(1)
84	
85	        assert status != "queued", (
86	            "job stayed 'queued' — the async drain never ran under `python -m backend.main` "
87	            "(the __main__/backend.main double-module entrypoint bug regressed)."
88	        )
89	    finally:
90	        proc.terminate()
91	        try:
92	            proc.wait(timeout=10)
93	        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
94	            proc.kill()
95	        log.close()
96

feat(pkg): P0a — resolved app-data HOME (RALLY_APP_HOME) for config/.env/DB/output (default-OFF)

PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0a. Introduce backend/utils/app_home.py — ONE resolver every mutable-state
path roots on — so a packaged app launched from an arbitrary CWD finds the same
config.json/.env/SQLite-DB/output, and `--setup` (doctor) writes config to the SAME place the
server reads it. Previously doctor wrote REPO_ROOT while the server read the CWD — a silent
data-loss footgun when launched from an OS menu.

HARD CONTRACT — with RALLY_APP_HOME UNSET, behaviour is byte-identical to pre-P0a:
  * default_config_path() == Path("config.json")  (CWD-relative)
  * env_file_to_load()    == None  -> verbatim load_dotenv() frame walk-up (no new CWD-.env preference)
  * resolve_output_dir("output") == "output"  (verbatim; no pathlib normalisation of ./ , seps, trailing /)
With it SET, config/.env/DB/output all root under the home; nothing leaks to the launch CWD.

- backend/utils/app_home.py (new): app_home / app_path / default_config_path / default_env_path /
  env_file_to_load / resolve_output_dir + default_os_app_home (per-OS dir for the P2 launcher, NOT
  auto-selected). stdlib-only so the early main.py import stays pre-pydantic.
- config/loader.py: load/save resolve via default_config_path(); dropped the frozen
  DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH module constant (no consumers; a snapshot would diverge from RALLY_APP_HOME).
- main.py: load_dotenv(env_file_to_load()); static mount package-relative (CWD-independent) with a
  read-only-install-tree-safe makedirs guard; output_dir routed through resolve_output_dir at the
  server-start and --trim sites.
- scripts/doctor.py: writes config.json to default_config_path() (+ corrected the stale docstring).
- docs/CODE_MAP.md: reflect the resolver (drop the DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH line).
- tests: tests/test_app_home.py (byte-identical-when-unset, env honoring, per-OS dirs, loader
  integration, env_file_to_load, drive passthrough, package-relative static dir), tests/test_doctor.py,
  and a subprocess end-to-end in test_server_entrypoint.py proving the DB lands under RALLY_APP_HOME
  (not the launch CWD).

Full suite 1232 passed / 7 skipped, ruff + mypy clean, coverage 85.10%. 4-lens adversarial review
folded (load_dotenv byte-identity, resolve_output_dir verbatim-when-unset, untested main() wiring,
import-time makedirs guard).

description: Open PR for P0a
command: cd C:/Users/avidu/Projects/bhi-wt-p0a && gh pr create --base master --head feat/packaging-p0a-app-home --title "feat(pkg): P0a — resolved app-data HOME (RALLY_APP_HOME) for config/.env/DB/output (default-OFF)" --body "$(cat <<'BODY'
## What & why
**PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0a** — the first prerequisite for shipping the engine as a downloadable, batteries-included app. Introduces `backend/utils/app_home.py`: **ONE resolver** every piece of mutable state roots on, so a packaged app launched from an arbitrary working directory finds the same `config.json` / `.env` / SQLite DB / `output/`. It also **fixes a real latent data-loss footgun today**: `scripts/doctor.py` wrote `config.json` to the repo root while the server read it from the CWD — they disagree the moment CWD ≠ repo root.

## Hard contract: byte-identical when `RALLY_APP_HOME` is unset
This is **default-OFF**. With the env var unset, behaviour is byte-identical to pre-P0a:
- `default_config_path() == Path("config.json")` (CWD-relative)
- `env_file_to_load() == None` → verbatim `load_dotenv()` frame walk-up (does **not** newly prefer a stray CWD `.env`)
- `resolve_output_dir("output") == "output"` (verbatim — no pathlib normalisation of `./`, separators, or trailing slash)

With it **set** (the packaged launcher will pin it in P2), config/.env/DB/output all root under the home and nothing leaks to the launch CWD.

## Changes
- **`backend/utils/app_home.py`** *(new)* — `app_home` / `app_path` / `default_config_path` / `default_env_path` / `env_file_to_load` / `resolve_output_dir` + `default_os_app_home` (per-OS dir: `%APPDATA%` / `~/Library/Application Support` / `$XDG_DATA_HOME`, for the P2 launcher to export — **not** auto-selected). stdlib-only so the early `main.py` import stays pre-pydantic.
- **`backend/config/loader.py`** — load/save resolve via `default_config_path()`; dropped the frozen `DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH` constant (no consumers).
- **`backend/main.py`** — `load_dotenv(env_file_to_load())`; static mount package-relative (CWD-independent) with a read-only-install-tree-safe `makedirs` guard; `output_dir` routed through `resolve_output_dir` at the server-start + `--trim` sites.
- **`scripts/doctor.py`** — writes `config.json` to `default_config_path()` (same path the server reads) + corrected docstring.
- **`docs/CODE_MAP.md`** — reflect the resolver.
- **tests** — `tests/test_app_home.py` (byte-identical-when-unset, env honoring, per-OS dirs, loader integration, `env_file_to_load`, drive passthrough, package-relative static dir), `tests/test_doctor.py`, and a **subprocess end-to-end** in `tests/test_server_entrypoint.py` proving the DB lands under `RALLY_APP_HOME` (not the launch CWD).

## Verification (local)
- Full suite **1232 passed / 7 skipped** (the optional obsreport tests), `ruff` + `mypy` clean.
- Coverage **85.10%** (CI floor 70; no regression) — `app_home.py` 100%, `loader.py` 97%.
- **4-lens adversarial review** folded: `load_dotenv` byte-identity (was preferring a CWD `.env`), `resolve_output_dir` verbatim-when-unset, untested `main()` wiring, and the import-time `makedirs` guard.

## Scope / not in this PR
The per-OS app dir is computed but **not** auto-selected — the launcher (P2) exports `RALLY_APP_HOME`. The worker's own output paths and the SPA bundling are later milestones. No behaviour changes for any existing user.

### 321. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.457Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
I have everything needed. Here are my findings.

## Findings

**[minor] backend/utils/redact.py:20 — Under-masking: several realistic secret-shaped names slip the regex.** The narrow allowlist misses `private_key`, `bearer`/`bearer_token`, `access_key`, `aws_access_key_id`, `signing_key`/`encryption_key`, `session_token`, `gh_token`, `authorization`, `cookie`. Notably `api_token` and `gemini_key` (a plausible variant of the very example the docstring/test centers on) both MISS — only `api_key`/`api-key`/`access_token` match, not `*_token` generally or `*_key` generally. This is a defensive future-proofing layer, so a miss only bites if someone later adds such a field AND it isn't caught by the accompanying test. Acceptable for P0b's stated narrow-on-purpose intent, but worth widening. Concrete fix: add alternations `|token|private[_-]?key|bearer|access[_-]?key|signing[_-]?key|encryption[_-]?key|authorization|cookie` (accepting that bare `token` would also mask `auth_token` variants and could catch a future non-secret `*token*` field — tune against the real field list).

**[nit] backend/utils/redact.py:42-43 — tuples are silently converted to lists.** `redact_secrets((1,2))` returns `[1,2]`. Irrelevant for the `/api/config` JSON path (Pydantic `model_dump(mode="json")` already emits lists), so no live impact; only matters if the helper is reused elsewhere on tuple-bearing data. Concrete fix: none required for this PR; note in the docstring that tuples normalize to lists, or `return type(obj)(...)` if tuple-preservation is ever wanted.

**No over-masking (verified):** the regex matches **zero** of the real AppConfig field names — enumerated all fields across AppConfig + ValidationConfig/relevance, TrackNet, WasbIndex, Fusion, Indexing, Output, Watermark, Stitching, Logging, Security, Storage, Deployment, Billing. In particular `cf_access_aud` (public id), `edge_auth_enabled` (bool), `auth`-adjacent names, `access_*` (e.g. none present), `*token*` (none present), `credential`/`secret` substrings (none present), and `*_key` knobs (none present) all pass through unmasked. The `cf_access_aud` field contains "access" but not "access_token", so it correctly does NOT match — no false positive.

**Serialization equivalence (verified):** `global_config.model_dump(mode="json")` is byte-equal to the old `return global_config` path (`jsonable_encoder(model)`) for every non-secret field — confirmed by direct comparison. All config fields are str/int/float/bool/list/dict/`Literal`; `Literal` values serialize as their plain string in both paths, and there are no `Path`/`datetime`/`Enum`/`set` fields that would diverge. So the response shape for real fields is unchanged.

**Test validity (verified):** `AppConfig(gemini_api_key=...)` is accepted via `model_config extra="allow"` (models.py:411), and `model_dump(mode="json")` surfaces the extra key — so the injected `gemini_api_key` is genuinely present pre-redaction and the test's `== "***REDACTED***"` assertion exercises a real masking, not a no-op.

**Leak-path analysis (clean):** A secret-named key masks its whole value before recursing (redact.py:37-38), so a structured secret (nested dict/list under e.g. `credentials`) can't escape via recursion — covered by `test_secret_named_key_masks_whole_nested_value`. Lists of dicts recurse correctly. The `None`-guard returns `{}` (main.py:166-167), leaking nothing. The one genuine residual gap is values where the secret is in the VALUE not the KEY — e.g. a connection string in a non-secret-named field like `storage.gcs = {"endpoint_url": "https://user:pass@host"}` or `input_root="gs://...@..."`. Redaction is key-name-based only, so such a value would pass through. This matches the documented design (config "stores no secrets… boto3/google auth chains supply credentials", models.py:317) and is out of scope for a key-name redactor; flagging only for completeness, not as a defect of this PR.

**Verdict: LGTM with minor follow-up** — correct, no over-m...

## What & why
**PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0b.** `GET /api/config` returns the **entire `AppConfig` with no auth**. Today AppConfig holds **no secrets** by design (the Gemini key is env-only; `cf_access_aud` is a public identifier), so this is a **defensive, fail-closed** hardening: if a credential-shaped field is ever added to the config, it is masked in the response and a test fails loudly rather than leaking it to any localhost process/browser tab.

## Changes
- **`backend/utils/redact.py`** *(new)* — `redact_secrets(obj)` deep-masks any value under a secret-shaped key (`password`/`secret`/`credential`/`api_key`/`*_token`/`*_key`/`bearer`/`authorization`/…). A secret-named key masks its **whole** value so a structured secret can't leak via recursion.
- **`backend/main.py`** — `get_config()` returns `redact_secrets(global_config.model_dump(mode="json"))` (`{}` if unset) instead of the raw model. **Byte-compatible JSON** for the real (non-secret) fields (verified).
- **tests** — `tests/test_redact.py` (masks the common secret names; does **not** over-mask `edge_auth_enabled` / `cf_access_aud` / `max_tokens` / `tokenizer_name`; recursion; whole-value mask; **a full-`AppConfig` over-masking guard that auto-updates as the config evolves**) + an `/api/config` endpoint test proving an injected `gemini_api_key` is masked while non-secret fields still serialize.
- `tests/test_config.py` — the typed-config-reaches-pipeline test now asserts `global_config` is the typed model **and** the handler returns a redacted dict view (the new contract).

## Verification (local)
- Full suite **1240 passed / 7 skipped**, `ruff` + `mypy` clean, coverage **85.12%** (floor 70), `redact.py` 100%.
- **Adversarial review** folded: confirmed zero over-masking against the enumerated real fields and `model_dump` byte-compatibility, then **widened** the matcher to `private_key`/`bearer`/`*_token`/`*_key`/`access_key`/`signing_key`/`authorization` after verifying no real field is hit (masking-more is the safe direction for an unauthenticated endpoint).

## Scope
The **Host-header / DNS-rebinding guard** is deferred to **P0c**, where it pairs with the bind-address + CORS exposure posture. No behaviour change for the real config fields.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

55	def _cors_origins_from_env(env: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = None) -> List[str]:
56	    """M9: CORS allow-origins from the ``RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS`` env var (comma-separated),
57	    default ``["*"]``. Read from the ENV (not a cwd ``config.json``) so importing ``backend.main``
58	    does zero filesystem I/O — a hosted/multi-tenant deploy sets this to lock CORS to its origin."""
59	    src = os.environ if env is None else env
60	    raw = (src.get("RALLY_CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS", "") or "").strip()
61	    origins = [o.strip() for o in raw.split(",") if o.strip()]
62	    return origins or ["*"]
63	
64	
65	# CORS for the web UI. allow_origins='*' with allow_credentials=True is rejected by browsers per the
66	# fetch spec + is unsafe to carry into multi-tenant; this tool uses no cookies, so credentials stay
67	# off. The origin list is configurable via the env var above (default ["*"]) so a hosted deploy can
68	# lock it to its origin; the middleware is fixed at startup.
69	app.add_middleware(
70	    CORSMiddleware,
71	    allow_origins=_cors_origins_from_env(),
72	    allow_credentials=False,
73	    allow_methods=["*"],
74	    allow_headers=["*"],

There's no standalone operator doc. The footgun surfacing rests entirely on the runtime print warning.

I have everything I need. Let me do a final precise check on the `getattr` null-safety in `_resolve_bind_host` (concern #4) — confirm `_resolve_bind_host(None, None)` works (already tested, returns `127.0.0.1`) and that `getattr(sec, "edge_auth_enabled", False)` when `sec` is `None` returns `False` safely.

`getattr(None, "edge_auth_enabled", False)` → `False` (safe; the test `test_bind_no_config_is_localhost` proves it). `getattr(config, "security", None)` when `config=None` → `None`. Both chains are null-safe.

Let me compile the findings.

## Review findings

**[minor] backend/main.py:90 (`_allowed_hosts_from_env`) + Starlette trustedhost.py:40 — IPv6 loopback Host headers are silently rejected.** The default allowlist includes `"::1"` and `"[::1]"`, but Starlette's `TrustedHostMiddleware` matches with `host = headers.get("host","").split(":")[0]`. A real IPv6 Host header is `"[::1]:8000"` → `.split(":")[0]` yields `"["` (and bare `"::1"` → `""`), neither of which matches any allowlist entry. Empirically verified: `[::1]:8000`, `[::1]`, and `::1` all return **400**. So if a user binds/visits the appliance over IPv6 loopback, every request is wrongly rejected. The `"::1"`/`"[::1]"` entries in the default are dead — they can never match given Starlette's port-stripping. Fix: document that IPv6 loopback is unsupported by this guard, or (cleaner) front it with a host-normalizer. The prompt flagged this as minor and it is — IPv4 loopback (the actual default bind `127.0.0.1`) works fine.

**[minor] backend/config/startup.py:91-139 vs backend/main.py:96-100 — the edge-auth exposure footgun is surfaced only as a soft print, not enforced like its sibling checks.** When `edge_auth_enabled=True`, `_resolve_bind_host` returns `0.0.0.0` (main.py:114-115) but the TrustedHost allowlist is frozen at import from `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` only (main.py:98) — it cannot see config. If `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset, the loopback-only default 400s **100%** of forwarded (public-Host) requests from the edge gate. The `_exposure_checks` startup gate already FATALs on a missing `allowed_video_root` and missing `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud`, but does **not** check the host allowlist — so a half-configured exposure (host-allowlist unset) starts "healthy" then 400s every real request. The only mitigation is the bind-time `print()` warning (main.py:823-827), which does correctly fire for `0.0.0.0` (verified: `0.0.0.0` is not in the suppress-tuple). Fix: add an `include_exposure` startup check that warns/errors when `edge_auth_enabled` and `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset/loopback-only, mirroring the existing `EXPOSED_*` checks — that's the cleaner option than the env-only docstring note. Not a blocker because the default path is unaffected and the print does surface it.

**[nit] backend/main.py:822 — default server-start banner string changed from `localhost` to the resolved bind host.** Was `http://localhost:{port}`; now `http://{bind_host}:{port}` which prints `http://127.0.0.1:8000` on the default path. The *bind* is byte-identical (`uvicorn.run` got `127.0.0.1` before too), but the user-visible banner text changed `localhost`→`127.0.0.1`. Purely cosmetic; flagging only because the contract says "byte-identical." No functional impact.

### Items checked and CLEARED (no issue)
- **Concern 1 — legitimate access:** `testserver` is allowlisted (main.py:90) → full TestClient suite passes (247 API tests + 10 new = green). Loopback Host **with port** `127.0.0.1:8000` → **200** (Starlette strips the port at trustedhost.py:40). Health, static `/`, and a good-host CORS preflight all return 200. No legitimate IPv4 path is wrongly 400'd.
- **Concern 3 — middleware...

[external_agent_tool_result]
90	def _exposure_checks(config: Any) -> list[StartupCheck]:
91	    """Exposure-safety posture for an *exposed* HTTP server. Gated on ``security.edge_auth_enabled``
92	    (default False → ``[]`` = strict no-op, today's behaviour). When edge auth is ON — the "I am
93	    exposing this engine behind the CF-Access gate" signal — the gate alone isn't enough: verify the
94	    rest of the posture at boot so a half-configured exposure fails fast instead of leaking / 500-ing
95	    per request. Config-only (no IO beyond the ``find_spec`` auth-extra probe)."""
96	    sec = getattr(config, "security", None)
97	    if not sec or not getattr(sec, "edge_auth_enabled", False):
98	        return []  # DEFAULT-OFF: edge auth off → no posture enforcement (byte-identical to today).
99	
100	    out: list[StartupCheck] = []
101	
102	    # (1) The path-jail is load-bearing once authenticated tenants can hit /api/process: WITHOUT it,
103	    # a tenant can point the engine at an ARBITRARY local file (allowed_video_root unset = "any local
104	    # file", the single-user default). Fatal for an exposed engine.
105	    root = getattr(sec, "allowed_video_root", None)
106	    if not root or not str(root).strip():
107	        out.append(StartupCheck(
108	            LEVEL_ERROR, "EXPOSED_NO_VIDEO_ROOT",
109	            "security.edge_auth_enabled is ON (engine exposed) but security.allowed_video_root is "
110	            "unset — an authenticated tenant could make /api/process read an arbitrary local file. "
111	            "Set allowed_video_root to a jail directory before exposing the engine.",
112	        ))
113	
114	    # (2) Edge auth cannot verify a token without the team domain + AUD. Today that only fails on the
115	    # FIRST request (auth.resolve_tenant); lift it to BOOT so a misconfigured gate never starts
116	    # "healthy" then 401s every request.
117	    team = (getattr(sec, "cf_team_domain", "") or "").strip()
118	    aud = (getattr(sec, "cf_access_aud", "") or "").strip()
119	    if not team or not aud:
120	        missing = " + ".join(n for n, v in (("cf_team_domain", team), ("cf_access_aud", aud)) if not v)
121	        out.append(StartupCheck(
122	            LEVEL_ERROR, "EXPOSED_EDGE_AUTH_INCOMPLETE",
123	            f"security.edge_auth_enabled is ON but {missing} is not configured — the Cf-Access JWT "
124	            "cannot be verified (every request would fail). Set security.cf_team_domain "
125	            "(e.g. https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com) and security.cf_access_aud (the CF Access "
126	            "application AUD tag).",
127	        ))
128	
129	    # (3) The auth extra (PyJWT[crypto]) is lazy-imported by auth.verify_cf_access_token. If it's
130	    # missing, every request 500s at verify time — a boot WARN beats a per-request 500. Warning, not
131	    # fatal: the auth module's own ImportError is the final authority.
132	    if not _auth_extra_available():
133	        out.append(StartupCheck(
134	            LEVEL_WARN, "EXPOSED_AUTH_EXTRA_MISSING",
135	            "security.edge_auth_enabled is ON but PyJWT[crypto] is not importable — Cf-Access "
136	            "verification will fail at request time. Install the 'auth' extra (pip install -e .[auth]).",
137	        ))
138	
139	    return out
140	
141

153	def run_startup_checks(
154	    config: Any,
155	    *,
156	    cuda_available: Optional[bool] = None,
157	    env: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
158	    include_exposure: bool = False,
159	) -> list[StartupCheck]:
160	    """Return the startup findings for ``config``.
161	
162	    ``cuda_available`` is the caller's best-effort GPU probe (``None`` = not probed → GPU checks
163	    are skipped; the API server passes ``None``, the worker passes ``presets.probe_cuda_available()``).
164	    ``include_exposure`` adds the exposure-safety posture (``_exposure_checks``) — the HTTP **server**
165	    passes ``True``; the worker leaves it ``False`` (it is never "exposed"). Both dimensions are
166	    DEFAULT-OFF: no profile → no profile checks; ``edge_auth_enabled=False`` → no exposure checks.
167	    Pure: no IO (beyond the ``find_spec`` auth probe), no torch import — the caller owns the GPU probe.
168	    """
169	    e = os.environ if env is None else env
170	    checks: list[StartupCheck] = []
171	
172	    # Exposure-safety posture — INDEPENDENT of deployment.profile (a tunnel-to-a-box exposure often
173	    # runs with no profile set), gated on edge_auth_enabled (default-OFF). Server-only.
174	    if include_exposure:
175	        checks.extend(_exposure_checks(config))
176	
177	    profile = getattr(getattr(config, "deployment", None), "profile", "") or ""
178	    if not profile:
179	        return checks  # no profile → only the exposure posture (itself off unless edge auth is on).
180	
181	    storage_backend = getattr(getattr(config, "storage", None), "backend", "local")
182	    indexing = getattr(config, "indexing", None)
183	    detector_impl = getattr(indexing, "detector_impl", "auto")
184	    device = getattr(getattr(indexing, "wasb", None), "device", "cuda")
185	
186	    if profile == "truly-local":
187	        # truly-local reads bytes from the local FS or a *mounted* Drive — a cloud backend here is
188	        # an inconsistent (but not fatal) choice worth surfacing.
189	        if storage_backend not in ("local", "gdrive"):
190	            checks.append(StartupCheck(
191	                LEVEL_WARN, "STORAGE_UNUSUAL",
192	                f"profile 'truly-local' usually uses local/gdrive storage, but storage.backend="
193	                f"'{storage_backend}'. Reads will go to a remote backend.",
194	            ))
195	        # GPU heads-up (warning only — the detector healthcheck is the authority; the probe is
196	        # best-effort and may be False even when a subprocess detector env has CUDA).
197	        if cuda_available is False:
198	            if device == "cuda":
199	                checks.append(StartupCheck(
200	                    LEVEL_WARN, "CUDA_NOT_VISIBLE",
201	                    "device='cuda' but no CUDA GPU is visible to this process. If the box truly "
202	                    "has no GPU the detector healthcheck will hard-fail — set indexing.wasb.device="
203	                    "'auto' for the CPU fallback (M4).",
204	                ))
205	            elif device == "auto":

echo "=== status (only P0c files) ===" && git status --porcelain

git commit -q -F - <<'MSG'
feat(pkg): P0c — config/profile-driven bind address + DNS-rebinding Host-header guard (default-OFF)

PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0c. The server hardcoded uvicorn host="127.0.0.1" and had no Host-header
validation. This makes the bind address profile-driven and adds a TrustedHostMiddleware guard,
both behaviour-identical on the default (localhost) path.

- Bind host: new `_resolve_bind_host(config, --host)` — precedence --host > RALLY_BIND_HOST env >
  0.0.0.0 when security.edge_auth_enabled (behind a CF-Access edge gate, M9) > 127.0.0.1 (default,
  today's behaviour). New `--host` CLI arg; a loud bind-time warning when exposing a routable iface.
- DNS-rebinding guard: TrustedHostMiddleware with an env-driven allowlist
  (`_allowed_hosts_from_env`, RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS) defaulting to loopback + Starlette's TestClient
  host "testserver" — so a malicious page that points a domain at 127.0.0.1 gets its Host header
  rejected (400). Read from ENV (no file I/O at import), mirroring the CORS knob. (IPv6 loopback is
  not supported by Starlette's host parsing — documented; use 127.0.0.1/localhost.)
- Startup parity: new WARN `EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK` in startup._exposure_checks when
  edge_auth_enabled is ON but RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS is unset (else the guard would 400 every forwarded
  request) — mirrors the existing EXPOSED_* checks; threaded `env` into _exposure_checks so it's
  testable.
- tests: tests/test_bind_and_host.py (bind precedence, allowlist resolution, and the middleware
  actually 400s a foreign Host / 200s loopback+testserver) + a startup test for the new WARN; the 6
  existing exact-match exposure tests now pass RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS (orthogonal to their dimension).

Default path is byte-identical: bind 127.0.0.1, testserver allowlisted (full TestClient suite green),
zero import-time I/O. Full suite 1271 passed / 7 skipped, ruff + mypy clean, coverage 85.13%.
Adversarial review folded (IPv6-loopback limitation documented; edge-auth host-allowlist footgun
promoted from a runtime print to a startup check).

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
MSG

### 451. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.466Z)

description: Open PR for P0c
command: cd C:/Users/avidu/Projects/bhi-wt-p0c && gh pr create --base master --head feat/packaging-p0c-bind-host --title "feat(pkg): P0c — config/profile-driven bind + DNS-rebinding Host-header guard (default-OFF)" --body "$(cat <<'BODY'
## What & why
**PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0c.** The server hardcoded `uvicorn.run(host="127.0.0.1")` and performed **no Host-header validation**. This makes the bind address **config/profile-driven** and adds a **DNS-rebinding guard** — both **behaviour-identical on the default (localhost) path**.

## Changes
- **Bind host** — `_resolve_bind_host(config, --host)`: precedence `--host` > `RALLY_BIND_HOST` env > `0.0.0.0` when `security.edge_auth_enabled` (i.e. behind the CF-Access edge gate, M9) > `127.0.0.1` (default, today's behaviour). New `--host` CLI arg + a loud bind-time warning when exposing a routable interface.
- **DNS-rebinding guard** — `TrustedHostMiddleware` with an env-driven allowlist (`_allowed_hosts_from_env`, `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS`) defaulting to loopback + Starlette's TestClient host `testserver`. A malicious page that points a domain at `127.0.0.1` gets its `Host` header **rejected (400)**. Read from ENV (no import-time I/O), mirroring the existing CORS knob. *(IPv6 loopback isn't matched by Starlette's host parsing — documented; use `127.0.0.1`/`localhost`.)*
- **Startup parity** — new WARN `EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK` in `startup._exposure_checks` when `edge_auth_enabled` is ON but `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset (else the guard would 400 every forwarded request), mirroring the existing `EXPOSED_*` checks. Threaded `env` into `_exposure_checks` so it's testable.
- **tests** — `tests/test_bind_and_host.py` (bind precedence; allowlist resolution; **the middleware actually 400s a foreign Host and 200s loopback/testserver**) + a startup test for the new WARN. The 6 existing exact-match exposure tests now pass `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (orthogonal to the dimension each tests).

## Verification (local)
- Default path **byte-identical**: bind `127.0.0.1`, `testserver` allowlisted (full TestClient suite green), zero import-time I/O.
- Full suite **1271 passed / 7 skipped**, `ruff` + `mypy` clean, coverage **85.13%** (floor 70), `startup.py` 97%.
- **Adversarial review** folded: confirmed the global middleware breaks no existing test, CORS/middleware order correct (`www_redirect=False`), null-safe resolver; then documented the IPv6-loopback limitation and **promoted** the edge-auth + missing-`RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` footgun from a runtime print to a boot-time startup WARN.

## Operator note
A network-exposed deploy (`edge_auth_enabled` → binds `0.0.0.0`) **must** set `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` to its public host(s) (or `*` when the edge gate validates the host) — now surfaced both at boot (startup WARN) and at bind time.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
BODY
)" 2>&1 | tail -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

  the SERVING path (backend/features/rally_seq.py: uniform_filter1d / maximum_filter1d) but was only
  present TRANSITIVELY via supervision — a supervision bump or a freeze that prunes unreferenced
  transitives would silently break inference. (BSD-3 — commercial-clean.)
- New CI lane `license-scan`: installs the RUNTIME closure (`pip install .`, no torch/dev) + 
  pip-licenses, prints the report, and FAILS on any copyleft (GPL/AGPL/LGPL/SSPL) — automating the
  MIT/Apache/BSD-only guardrail that was human-only. Verified the closure is all permissive 
  (MIT/Apache/BSD/0BSD/Zlib/CC0); the only AGPL packages locally (ultralytics, PyMuPDF) are NOT in
  the closure.
- New CI lane `build-artifact`: builds the wheel, installs it NON-editable + CPU torch, and launches
  `indexer --help` + imports backend/training from a non-repo cwd. Catches packaging regressions the
  source suite can't — namespace-package resolution (backend has no __init__.py), missing data files
  (e.g. the bundled font), hidden imports. Validated locally end-to-end in a clean venv.

No Python/behaviour change. Full suite 1271 passed / 7 skipped; ci.yml YAML validated; wheel build +
clean-venv install + `indexer --help` proven locally.

It's imported **directly on the serving path** (`backend/features/rally_seq.py`: `uniform_filter1d` / `maximum_filter1d`) but was only present **transitively** via `supervision` — a `supervision` bump or a freeze that prunes unreferenced transitives would silently break inference. - **New CI lane `build-artifact`** — builds the wheel, installs it **non-editable** + CPU torch, then launches `indexer --help` and imports `backend`/`training` **from a non-repo cwd**. Catches packaging regressions the source suite can't see: namespace-package resolution (`backend` has no `__init__.py`), missing data files (the bundled font), hidden imports.

- Built the wheel and confirmed it includes `backend/main.py`, `training/`, `backend/utils/app_home.py`, and `backend/assets/fonts/RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf`.

- `pip-licenses` over the runtime deps → all permissive; the `grep -iE "GPL|SSPL"` denylist finds nothing in the closure.

### 570. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.475Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
One more consideration on the lock lifecycle: on the CLI single-shot path, main() returns normally (e.g. line 769, 837) without ever calling `release_lock`. The lock relies on the OS releasing it at process exit. That's fine and documented — but worth confirming there's no in-process workflow where main() returns and the process keeps running (e.g. main() called twice in one process, or a test importing main). The subprocess tests spawn fresh processes, so fine. In-process, `_instance_lock` would stay held until process exit; a second `main()` call in the same process would `acquire_lock` the same path on a *new* handle — which on POSIX (flock) is associated per-open-file-description so a new `open()` + flock would **block/fail** against the same process's existing lock (flock conflicts across different fds even in the same process), and on Windows msvcrt also conflicts across handles. This only matters for a hypothetical double-`main()`-in-one-process, not a real workflow. Noting as informational, not a finding.

I have enough to write the review.

---

## Findings

**[minor] backend/utils/single_instance.py:35 — `open(lock_path, "a+")` is OUTSIDE the try/except.** If the lock directory/path is not writable (read-only app-home, permission error, path is a directory), `open()` raises `OSError`/`PermissionError` which propagates out of `acquire_lock` and crashes `main()` with an unhandled traceback — instead of the clean `[FATAL] … Refusing to start` message the design intends for the "can't run here" case. `os.makedirs(..., exist_ok=True)` at line 34 can raise too. — **Fix:** wrap the `makedirs`+`open` in try/except and either return `None` (caller already prints FATAL + exits) or raise a typed error main() catches; at minimum document that acquire_lock can raise on an unwritable path so the caller isn't surprised.

**[nit] backend/utils/single_instance.py:20,26 — unparameterized `IO` generic** (`Optional[IO]`). mypy is reported clean (bare `IO` defaults to `IO[Any]`), so this is cosmetic, but `IO[str]` is the precise type for an `"a+"`/text-mode handle. — **Fix (optional):** annotate `Optional[IO[str]]`.

**[nit] backend/utils/single_instance.py:51 — PID written without a trailing newline / encoding is platform-default.** `fh.write(str(os.getpid()))` is best-effort diagnostic only (the comment says so) and the test reads it back with `.strip()`, so harmless. Mentioned only for completeness; not worth changing.

### Verified clean (no findings)
- **Race prevention / ordering** — lock acquired at main.py:743, `None`→`sys.exit(1)` at 744-748, **before** `Database()` (750) and `fail_stale_jobs()` (754). The destructive sweep cannot run until the lock is held. Correct.
- **fd kept alive** — `_instance_lock` is a module global (main.py:148), assigned under `global _instance_lock` (742); never GC'd → lock held for process lifetime. Correct.
- **OS release on crash/exit** — `fcntl.flock` (POSIX, line 43) and `msvcrt.locking` byte-range lock (Windows, line 40) are both kernel-managed and released when the handle closes or the process dies. The docstring's "no stale-lock problem" claim is accurate.
- **CLI-mode implication** — lock is intentionally before the mode branch (738-748 precede the `if args.video_path and not args.server` at 759), so CLI single-shot *and* `--server` both lock. A CLI run while a server is up correctly refuses (it would otherwise sweep the server's jobs). No `release_lock` on the CLI return paths, but OS-on-exit covers it. **No stale lock file on disk matters** — the lock is the open-handle, not file contents; the leftover `.lock` file is harmless and re-lockable.
- **Different output-dirs coexist** — lock path is `db_path + ".lock"` (743) and `db_path` = `join(output_dir, db_name)` (736); distinct output-dirs → distinct lock paths → no conflict. Covered by test_single_instance.py:30 and the integration test (test_server_entrypoint.py:136 uses same dir to prove refusal).
- **msvcrt write-after-lock** — locks...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## What & why
**PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0e** (final P0 prerequisite). The serving model runs an **in-process** job worker, and `fail_stale_jobs()` sweeps `queued`/`running` jobs to `failed` at startup. A **second** process opening the same SQLite DB (a 2nd `indexer --server`, or a CLI run while a server is up) would sweep the **first** process's in-flight jobs — corrupting live state. This adds a single-instance guard.

## Changes
- **`backend/utils/single_instance.py`** *(new)* — `acquire_lock`/`release_lock`: an exclusive, **non-blocking OS lock** (`fcntl.flock` on POSIX, `msvcrt.locking` on Windows; branched on `sys.platform` for clean mypy platform-narrowing). Held via an open file handle for the process lifetime and **released by the OS on exit OR crash** — no stale-lock problem (unlike a bare PID file).
- **`backend/main.py`** — acquire the lock on `<db_path>.lock` **before** the destructive `fail_stale_jobs()` sweep; a 2nd instance against the same DB prints a clear `[FATAL]` and exits 1. Keyed to `db_path`, so a **different** `--output-dir`/`RALLY_APP_HOME` runs independently. An **unwritable lock path degrades to a WARN + proceed** (a lock-infra failure must not block a legitimate single instance, nor falsely claim a 2nd one).
- **tests** — `tests/test_single_instance.py` (acquire / second-acquire-blocked / release→reacquire / distinct-paths / parent-dir-created / pid-recorded) + a **subprocess integration test**: server 1 healthy → server 2 on the **same** `--output-dir` (different port) → exits non-zero with the FATAL message.

## Verification (local)
- Default path unchanged (a single instance acquires the lock + proceeds).
- Full suite **1277 passed / 7 skipped**, `ruff` + `mypy` clean, coverage **85.07%** (floor 70).
- **Adversarial review** folded: confirmed the race is closed (lock precedes the sweep), the fd lifecycle, OS-release-on-crash, and the CLI-mode implication are all correct; then added the graceful degrade on an unwritable lock path + a precise `IO[str]` annotation.

This completes the **P0 engine-prerequisite tranche** (P0a–P0e) from the packaging plan — the engine is now safe to launch from an arbitrary directory and to bundle.

### 605. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.477Z)

Pulled `khelsutra` (up to date, now at #67). Here's exactly what "touches the separate khelsutra repo" means, grounded in what I just verified:

## The two-repo split
- **The engine** = *this* repo, `Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer` — the Python/FastAPI backend and the wheel I've been hardening.
- **The actual UI you click** (per-rally grid, query bar, build→download, en/kn/hi) = a **different git repo**, `avidullu/khelsutra`, in its `web/` folder (Vite/React/TS). Different repo — different GitHub namespace even (`avidullu/` vs `Khelsutra/`), Cloudflare-connected, actively developed by the sibling sessions.
- What the engine serves today at `/` is a **legacy dead mini-dashboard** (`backend/api/static/{index.html,app.js,style.css}`), **not** that SPA.

## Why P1 unavoidably reaches across repos
Right now those run as **two processes** in dev — the Vite dev server proxies `/api` → engine:8000. But a *batteries-included download* must be **one thing**: the engine serves the real UI itself, single-origin (the SPA already calls same-origin `/api`, so no SPA code change — confirmed). To do that, the **engine wheel has to physically contain the built UI**.

The catch: the SPA's build output (`web/dist/`) is **gitignored in khelsutra** — it's produced by `npm run build`, committed nowhere. And we can't run `npm` on the user's machine at `pip install` time. So **at our package-build time, the SPA must already be built and copied into the engine tree** (e.g. `backend/ui/`, force-included into the wheel). That build-time dependency on a second, independently-developed repo *is* the cross-repo coupling. It's not something the engine repo can decide entirely on its own — and I don't want to unilaterally restructure your product/brand repo or its Cloudflare wiring.

So P1 splits cleanly:
- **P1a — engine-only, fully unblocked**: the 3 net-new endpoints (`/api/capabilities`, `/api/videos` list, `/api/reels`) + an `/api/version` handshake + contract test. No khelsutra involvement. **I'll start here now.**
- **P1b — the cross-repo bundling**: needs your call on *how* the SPA gets into the wheel.

That one decision is genuinely yours (it touches repo structure + your separate product repo), so:

=== add_video INSERT columns ===
    def add_video(self, video_id: str, filepath: str, status: str, validation_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
        """Register or update a video row WITHOUT touching its child segments.

        Uses UPSERT, not INSERT OR REPLACE: REPLACE deletes the conflicting row, which —
        with foreign_keys ON — cascade-deletes every play_segment/event/candidate_segment.
        That made a status update (e.g. marking 'failed' on re-validation, or 'processed'
        before the segmenter runs) silently destroy prior good results. UPSERT mutates the
        row in place; clearing segments is now an explicit, deliberate operation
        (clear_video_data / prune_segments).
        """
        return self._execute_write(
            "INSERT INTO videos (id, filepath, status, validation_results) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?) "
            "ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET "
            "filepath=excluded.filepath, status=excluded.status, "
            "validation_results=excluded.validation_results",
            (video_id, filepath, status, json.dumps(validation_results)),
            "Failed to add video",
        )

### 653. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.480Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
Path safety holds empirically:
- `..%2f..%2f...` (encoded `/`) → Starlette decodes `%2f` to `/` inside the single-segment param, then `safe_output_filename` would reject it — but here it 404s (the decoded `../../backend/main.py` doesn't match a `.mp4`/`.mov` and isn't a file in output_dir; the path containment + ext gate catch it). Either way, no escape.
- Null byte → 400 (rejected by `safe_output_filename`).
- Backslash → 400 (rejected).

Traversal is not reachable. Q2 is clean.

I have enough evidence for all six review axes. Here are my findings.

---

## Findings

**[minor] backend/main.py:49 and backend/main.py:261 — The inline comments `# cheap env-based GPU probe (no torch import)` and `cuda = probe_cuda_available()  # cheap, env-based (no torch import)` are factually false.** `probe_cuda_available()` → `presets.py:106 _cuda_available()` → `import torch; torch.cuda.is_available()` (presets.py:90-92). Measured: first `/api/capabilities` call takes ~3.5s (torch import + CUDA init); subsequent calls are cached (0ms). The function is correctly wrapped in try/except so it never raises and returns the right bool, but it is NOT cheap and NOT env-based on the first call. **Fix:** correct both comments to "best-effort CUDA probe (lazy torch import, ~3s first call, cached after; never raises)", or, if first-run-wizard latency matters, gate the torch probe behind a query param / cache it at startup.

**[minor] backend/infrastructure/database.py:480 — `list_videos` docstring claims "the videos table has no timestamp, so order by rowid", but the table DOES have `processed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (database.py:121).** The ordering choice itself is *correct and arguably better* — `id` is `TEXT PRIMARY KEY` so `rowid` is a true monotonic insertion counter, immune to the 1-second granularity ties `processed_at` would suffer. Only the stated rationale is wrong. **Fix:** reword to "order by rowid (monotonic insertion order; more reliable than the 1s-granularity processed_at for newest-first)".

**[nit] backend/main.py:269 — `/api/capabilities` serializes full `ffmpeg_path` / `ffprobe_path` on an unauthenticated endpoint, disclosing the install layout (and OS username on Windows paths).** This is *consistent* with the established posture of the sibling localhost endpoints — `/api/videos` returns `filepath` (full source paths) and `/api/config` returns `output.output_dir` — so it is an acceptable disclosure under the current single-user-localhost threat model, not a regression. No secret value or full secret path is leaked: `gemini_key_present` is `bool(os.environ.get("GEMINI_API_KEY"))` and `wasb_weights_present` is `bool(weights_path) and os.path.exists(weights_path)` — both pure bools, neither the key nor the weights path crosses the wire (verified by the test asserting `"AIza-should-not-leak" not in r.text`). **Fix (optional):** if exposure tightens later, drop `ffmpeg_path`/`ffprobe_path` to bools-only like the secret fields; fine to defer.

### Axes that are clean (no issue)
- **Q1 SECRET LEAK:** Clean. Only booleans for key/weights presence; no key value, no full secret path. (`ffmpeg_path` disclosure noted above as a consistent nit.)
- **Q2 PATH SAFETY:** Clean. `safe_output_filename` rejects empty/null-byte/`/`/`\\`/absolute/`..`/non-basename; `resolve_under_root` (realpath + commonpath) is real defense-in-depth catching symlink escape; the `.mp4`/`.mov` ext gate is ANDed with `os.path.isfile`. Route is single-segment `{filename}` (not `{filename:path}`) so separators don't span. Empirically probed encoded-`/`, encoded-`..`, null-byte, backslash → all 400/404, no escape. `os.scandir` handle is closed via `with entries:` (main.py:316). No exploitable TOCTOU (realpath resolved, then `isfile`, then `FileResponse` opens — worst case is a 404/serve of a since-replaced file under the same contained root, not an escape).
- **Q3 HONESTY:** Accurate. Confirmed `/api/compile` → `VideoCompiler(output_dir).compile_highlights`...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### 654. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.480Z)

The honest cheap signal for the wizard is `nvidia-smi` presence (what `doctor.py` uses). (2) the `list_videos` docstring claims "no timestamp" but the table has `processed_at`. Fixing both.

  video files only (per-video scratch dirs / DB / .lock excluded by the ext gate). Download mirrors
  /api/highlights' bare-name + containment safety.

## Adversarial review — PR P1b (bundle khelsutra SPA into engine wheel, single-origin)

**[nit] backend/main.py:67 / scripts/bundle_ui.sh:67 — `content_hash` is path-dependent, not a reproducible content fingerprint** — `CONTENT_HASH="$(find "$UI_DEST" ... | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum ...)"` runs `find` over the *absolute* `$UI_DEST` path, and the outer `sha256sum` hashes a stream that embeds those absolute paths (verified: it reproduces `fad6253…` only with the literal `/c/Users/avidu/Projects/bhi-wt-p1b/...` prefix; a relative-path recompute gives `ac2a3d6…`). So re-bundling identical bytes from any other checkout path/machine/CI yields a different hash. It's recorded as provenance but isn't a verifiable fingerprint, and no test asserts it. — Fix: hash with paths relativized to `$UI_DEST` (e.g. `cd "$UI_DEST" && find . -type f ! -name ui_version.json | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum`), or drop the field if it's only decorative.

**[nit] scripts/bundle_ui.sh:66 — `API_VERSION` extraction is brittle to a type annotation** — the anchor `^API_VERSION\s*=` matches today's `API_VERSION = 1` (verified → `1`), but a future `API_VERSION: int = 1` would not match → `ENGINE_API_VERSION` empty → json written as `engine_api_version: null`. This fails *safe* (the skew test then asserts `null == 1` and fails loudly, prompting a re-bundle), so it's not a guard hole — just a footgun. — Fix: loosen to `^API_VERSION\b[^=]*=` or note the assumption.

**[minor] backend/ui/assets/*.js + *.woff2 — minified third-party JS (React etc.) + OFL Noto fonts are committed into the MIT engine repo, and the license-scan lane does not cover them** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml:142` runs `pip-licenses` (Python-only); it cannot see the JS/font blobs. No `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` is vendored alongside the bundle (verified: none under `backend/ui/`). Given the project's hard MIT/Apache/BSD-only guardrail for *all* deps, and that OFL/MIT carry attribution obligations, this warrants an explicit note in the PR (and ideally a vendored third-party-notices file from the SPA build). Not a serving/correctness blocker. — Fix: PR should NOTE the JS/font licensing posture; consider vendoring the SPA's generated licenses file into `backend/ui/`.

**Verified clean (no issue):**
- **Serving correctness** — `serve_index` (main.py:151) reads `_UI_DIR/index.html`; `/assets` mount (main.py:147) is guarded by `os.path.isdir(_UI_ASSETS_DIR)` (146), registered at import only when the dir exists. No `/assets`-prefixed API route exists → no conflict; `/static` (136) is a distinct prefix. Fallback when `backend/ui` absent: `_UI_DIR` → `_STATIC_DIR` (144), legacy `index.html` references `/static/*` served by the always-present `/static` mount (verified functional, no crash); `_UI_ASSETS_DIR` becomes a non-existent `static/assets` so `/assets` is simply not mounted. Traversal: Starlette 1.0.0 `StaticFiles` normalizes and 404s `../` escapes.
- **Route order / shadowing** — no `{path:path}` catch-all anywhere (main.py + `backend/api/uploads.py` router routes are all `/api/upload/*`). Serving `index.html` at `/` (150) does not intercept `/api/*` or `/static`.
- **Bundler robustness** — `set -euo pipefail` (sh:17); option-3 uses `curl -fsSL` + `tar -xzf` into a `mktemp -d` with `trap … EXIT` cleanup; `stage_dist` validates `index.html` presence and does `rm -rf` + `mkdir -p` + `cp -r "$dist"/.` into a clean dest (correct). Untrusted-tarball traversal is the standard `tar` caveat but the source is the owner's own release artifact (acceptable). CI never bundles — no `.github/` reference to `bundle_ui`/`npm`/`backend/ui` (verified); the snapshot is committed.

[external_agent_tool_result]
All confirmed. The health endpoint is auth-free and the poll uses `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>` which passes the TrustedHostMiddleware default allowlist. Note one interaction worth flagging: if a user sets `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` without including `127.0.0.1`, the health poll would 400 — but the default keeps `127.0.0.1`, and `--app` forces 127.0.0.1 bind, so the default path is safe. Setting `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` to an exclusive non-loopback set while using `--app` is a self-inflicted misconfiguration; the health poll would then never see 200 and the browser simply wouldn't open (no dead page). Acceptable.

Here are my findings.

---

**Adversarial review — PR P2b (`indexer --app` launcher)**

**1. Mode interactions** — Clean. The CLI single-shot guard at `backend/main.py:970` (`if args.video_path and not args.server and not args.app`) correctly excludes `--app`, so `--video-path --app` never runs single-shot processing and falls through to the server block at `:1051` (`if args.app or args.server or not args.video_path`). `--app --server` → `--app` branch wins (`:1052` checks `args.app` first) → localhost-only, which is the safe precedence. No-args → server (`not args.video_path`). No combination runs two modes. The `--setup`/`--trim`/`--debug-clip` branches (`:872`,`:886`,`:918`) return early and require their own flags, independent of `--app`.

**2. Localhost invariant** — Confirmed. `:1055` hardcodes `bind_host = "127.0.0.1"` for `--app`, fully bypassing `_resolve_bind_host` / `--host` / `RALLY_BIND_HOST` / `edge_auth_enabled`. `--server` still honors the P0c profile via `_resolve_bind_host(global_config, args.host)` at `:1065`. Intended and safe — the friendly app can never expose the network.

**3. Browser-open thread** — Solid. Daemon thread (`:1062-1063` `daemon=True`) → won't block process exit or Ctrl+C. `RALLY_NO_BROWSER` honored at `:158`. Dead-page guard: the `while/else` at `:163-171` returns False without opening if health never returns 200 (no open on a server that fails to bind). `webbrowser.open` is wrapped in try/except (`:172-175`), exceptions swallowed. No race: it polls `/api/health` and only opens after a 200. Health URL is `url + "api/health"` = `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/health` (url ends in `/`, `:1059`) — `urllib` sends `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>`, which passes TrustedHostMiddleware (port stripped → `127.0.0.1`, in the default allowlist at `:100`). `/api/health` is auth-free (`:298`).

**4. Free-port (TOCTOU)** — Acceptable as documented. `_resolve_app_port` (`:149-151`) checks `_port_is_free` then uvicorn binds later. `_port_is_free` (`:131`) binds without `SO_REUSEADDR`; uvicorn binds with `SO_REUSEADDR=1` — a strictly-free port passes both, so the check is not falsely positive. `_find_free_port` (`:142`) returns an OS-assigned ephemeral port (free at that instant). Window is best-effort; uvicorn surfaces a real bind failure loudly (docstring `:132-133`). The resolved `port` is used consistently for BOTH the browser URL (`:1059`) and `uvicorn.run` (`:1077`).

**5. Single-instance (P0e)** — Correct. `--app` reaches `acquire_lock(db_path + ".lock")` at `:946` and `fail_stale_jobs()` at `:965` (no early return between `:924` and the lock — verified). A 2nd `--app` on the same `--output-dir` → lock fails → `[FATAL]` + `sys.exit(1)` at `:956-959`. Lock-infra failure (read-only dir) warns and proceeds without the guard (`:951`), not a crash.

**6. Resource/socket leaks** — None. Both `_port_is_free` (`:134`) and `_find_free_port` (`:144`) use `with socket.socket(...)` → sockets always closed. `urllib.request.urlopen` uses `with` (`:165`). `webbrowser.open` exceptions swallowed (`:174`).

**[nit] backend/main.py:95-100** — If a user sets `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` to an exclusively non-loopback set *and* uses `--app`, the health poll's `Host: 127.0.0.1` would 400 and the browser would silently never open (no dead page, no crash). Self-inflicted misconfiguration and the default path is safe; worth a one-line note th...

1	import subprocess
2	import logging
3	from typing import Optional
4	
5	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
6	
7	def get_video_duration(video_path: str) -> float:
8	    """Returns the exact duration of a video file in seconds using ffprobe.
9	    Returns 0.0 on failure."""
10	    try:
11	        duration_output = subprocess.check_output([
12	            "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
13	            "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1", video_path
14	        ]).decode().strip()
15	        return float(duration_output)
16	    except Exception as e:
17	        logger.error(f"Could not detect video duration for {video_path}: {e}")
18	        return 0.0
19	
20	def extract_video_chunk(video_path: str, start_time: float, duration: float,
21	                        output_path: str, reencode: bool = False,
22	                        scale_width: Optional[int] = None) -> bool:
23	    """Extracts a chunk from a video file.
24	
25	    Args:
26	        reencode: If True, re-encodes with libx264 (slower but frame-accurate).
27	                  If False, uses stream-copy (fast but cuts at nearest keyframe).
28	                  Use True for short clips where keyframe drift matters.
29	        scale_width: If set (requires reencode), downscale to this width (height auto,
30	                  even). Useful for high-res sources headed to an API that analyses at
31	                  low res anyway — shrinks uploads with no analysis loss.
32	    """
33	    cmd = [
34	        "ffmpeg", "-y",
35	        "-ss", str(start_time),
36	        "-t", str(duration),
37	        "-i", video_path,
38	    ]
39	    if reencode:
40	        if scale_width:
41	            cmd += ["-vf", f"scale={int(scale_width)}:-2"]
42	        cmd += ["-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", "ultrafast", "-crf", "23", "-c:a", "aac"]
43	    else:
44	        cmd += ["-c", "copy"]
45	    cmd.append(output_path)
46	    try:
47	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
48	        return True
49	    except Exception as e:
50	        logger.error(f"Failed to extract chunk {start_time}s - {start_time+duration}s: {e}")
51	        return False
52	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

3	Stages 0–1 of docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md: extract the audio track, build a
4	spectral-flux onset envelope (with pre-emphasis to boost the broadband HF transient
5	a racket–shuttle contact makes), threshold it adaptively, and return (timestamp,
6	strength) hit candidates. Pure numpy + stdlib `wave` (NO scipy); ffmpeg (subprocess)
7	only for extraction. Consumed by the E1 go/no-go probe; later feeds rally fusion
8	(ADR-007) if E1 passes. WASB is never modified — this is a separate signal.

10	from __future__ import annotations

12	import os
13	import wave
14	import tempfile

28	def extract_audio(video_path: str, out_wav: Optional[str] = None, sr: int = 16000) -> str:
29	    """ffmpeg → mono 16-bit PCM WAV at `sr`. Returns the wav path (temp if not given).
30	    Audio-only decode, so it's fast even for multi-GB video."""
31	    if out_wav is None:
32	        fd, out_wav = tempfile.mkstemp(suffix=".wav")
33	        os.close(fd)
34	    cmd = ["ffmpeg", "-y", "-i", video_path, "-vn", "-ac", "1", "-ar", str(sr),
35	           "-c:a", "pcm_s16le", out_wav]
36	    subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
37	    return out_wav

feat(pkg): P2a — single ffmpeg/ffprobe resolver (env-overridable; one fail-loud place) (default-OFF)

PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2a. Route every ffmpeg/ffprobe invocation through one resolver so a packaged app
can point at a vendored / non-PATH binary, there is ONE place for a config knob or a louder check,
and a missing ffmpeg fails with an actionable OS-specific install hint instead of a cryptic
FileNotFoundError from deep in the pipeline.

- backend/utils/ffmpeg.py (new): ffmpeg_bin()/ffprobe_bin() (RALLY_FFMPEG/RALLY_FFPROBE env override,
  else the bare name → PATH), ffmpeg_available()/ffprobe_available(), install_hint() (per-OS), and
  require_ffmpeg() (raises FfmpegNotFound with the hint). stdlib-only → no import cycle.
- Wrapped all 13 argv[0] call sites: compiler.py (x3), utils/video.py (x2), tools/rally_slicer.py
  (x2), segmenters/gemini.py, pipeline/audio/hit_detector.py, validators/format.py + pts_continuity.py,
  worker/__main__.py; and routed /api/capabilities' shutil.which through the resolver.
- DEFAULT (env unset) returns "ffmpeg"/"ffprobe" → byte-identical argv, so every subprocess-mocking
  test still passes — a pure seam, no behaviour change.

tests/test_ffmpeg_resolver.py: defaults/override/blank-env, availability, require_ffmpeg raise+noop,
per-OS hints, and a call-site wiring test (RALLY_FFPROBE flows through to argv[0]). Full suite 1312
passed / 7 skipped, ruff + mypy clean, coverage 85.12% (ffmpeg.py 100%); completeness grep confirms
no unwrapped literal remains. (First-run cpu-mock self-test deferred to the wizard/first-run slice.)

    @staticmethod
    def _add_column_idempotent(cursor: sqlite3.Cursor, ddl: str) -> None:
        """Apply a bare ``ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`` migration step re-runnably.

        A DDL ``ALTER`` auto-commits independently of the surrounding ``with conn:``
        transaction, so an interrupted v3/v4 block can leave the column added while
        ``user_version`` stays un-bumped — and the next open would re-run the same
        ``ALTER`` and raise ``OperationalError: duplicate column name``, bricking every
        future ``Database(...)`` construction (``_init_db`` runs in ``__init__``). Swallow
        ONLY that specific error so the ladder stays crash-safe, matching the re-runnable
        ``CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS`` v1/v2/v5 blocks.
        """
        try:
            cursor.execute(ddl)
        except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
            if "duplicate column name" not in str(exc).lower():
                raise

    def _init_db(self):
        """Initializes tables for videos, play_segments, and events."""
        with self._connect() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()

            self._create_base_tables(cursor)

            # Versioned migrations live here. PRAGMA user_version is the schema
            # contract — bump it for every change and add an ordered `if version < N`
            # block below. Tests assert the expected version, so accidental drift fails CI.
            version = cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0]

            if version < 1:
                self._migrate_to_v1(cursor)

            if version < 2:
                self._migrate_to_v2(cursor)

            if version < 3:
                self._migrate_to_v3(cursor)

            if version < 4:
                self._migrate_to_v4(cursor)

            if version < 5:
                self._migrate_to_v5(cursor)

            if version < 6:
                self._migrate_to_v6(cursor)

            if version < 7:
                self._migrate_to_v7(cursor)
            # future: if version < 8: cursor.execute("ALTER TABLE ..."); PRAGMA user_version = 8

            conn.commit()

class Database:
    def __init__(self, db_path: str):
        self.db_path = db_path
        self._init_db()

    @contextmanager
    def _connect(self):
        """Transaction scope that also CLOSES the connection.

        ``with sqlite3.connect(...)`` alone is only a commit/rollback scope — it
        never closes, so every call leaked its connection to GC (lingering file
        handles on the WAL sidecars under Windows). Internal methods use this.
        """
        conn = self._get_connection()
        try:
            with conn:
                yield conn
        finally:
            conn.close()

    def _get_connection(self):
        # busy_timeout: wait (not fail) on a locked DB — needed once the async job model
        # adds concurrent writers (a swallowed 'database is locked' silently drops segments).
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=30.0)
        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        # Enforce ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL — SQLite leaves FKs off per-connection.
        conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
        # WAL allows concurrent readers during a write; busy_timeout bounds lock waits.
        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 30000")
        try:
            conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
            pass  # e.g. a read-only/networked FS that can't do WAL — degrade gracefully
        return conn
