=== person_detector.py: imports + how torch is used ===
"""Person-detection rally cue — torchvision detector + ByteTrack + motion windowing.

Extracted verbatim from `yolo_hybrid` (fusion P1, `docs/MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN.md`):
the **person cue is a swappable producer**, not logic baked into one segmenter. Today
`HybridYoloSegmenter` is the only consumer (it delegates Stage-1 here, byte-identically);
next the fusion segmenter consumes the SAME producer over shuttle-seeded windows, and
later the learned classifier reads its persisted features. Keeping detection here (not in
the segmenter) is what makes "add a cue = register a producer" true.

Behaviour is unchanged from the in-lined version — only the home moved:
  - `lazy_load_model`               — load a permissively-licensed torchvision detector
  - `make_tracker`                  — a fresh supervision ByteTrack per chunk
  - `detect_and_track`              — one frame → confident persons with persistent IDs
  - `extract_action_windows_from_chunk` — a chunk → high-motion candidate windows
    with the motion stats (`peak/mean_velocity`, `active_frame_count`, `track_id_count`)
    that feed the candidate `stats` JSON and the learned fusion features.

The model libs (torchvision, supervision) are imported lazily inside the methods, and the
detector weights load only on first detection (`lazy_load_model`) — so importing the
segmenter registry never loads a detector model, and tests can mock the seams. (torch + cv2
are top-level imports here; CI's import-smoke stubs them, and they're present on the GPU
machine.) Licensing: torchvision detectors are BSD + COCO weights; ByteTrack is MIT
(ADR-005; ultralytics' AGPL YOLO was dropped).
"""
import logging
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import cv2
import torch

from backend.utils.geometry import get_velocity_normalised

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# torchvision detection models are trained on COCO where the "person" category is
# class 1 (index 0 is the implicit background). NB: ultralytics YOLO used class 0 —
# this off-by-one is the classic gotcha when swapping detectors. Keep it named.
_PERSON_CLASS_ID = 1

# Permissively-licensed torchvision detectors selectable via config
# `indexing.detector_model`. All are BSD-licensed (code + COCO weights).
_DETECTOR_FACTORIES = {
    "fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn": "fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn",
    "fasterrcnn_mobilenet_v3_large_fpn": "fasterrcnn_mobilenet_v3_large_fpn",
    "retinanet_resnet50_fpn": "retinanet_resnet50_fpn",

feat(pkg): P2e — lazy-torch so the LIGHT tier imports with NO torch installed (default-OFF)

PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2e. The plan's LIGHT tier (Gemini / motion / cpu-mock) ships no torch, but
`import backend.main` pulled torch at module load via the ONE eager top-level `import torch` in
person_detector.py — so the "no torch" promise didn't actually hold. This defers that import.

- person_detector.py: drop the top-level `import torch`; import it lazily inside the two methods
  that use it (`lazy_load_model`, `detect_and_track`) — alongside the already-lazy torchvision /
  supervision imports. cv2 stays top-level (opencv-python is a hard dep, always installed). The
  person cue is HEAVY-only (yolo/fusion) and never instantiated under LIGHT, so this is pure
  deferral with no behaviour change on the HEAVY path (torch present there).
- tests/test_light_tier_no_torch.py (new): spawns a fresh interpreter with torch/torchvision BLOCKED
  at the import machinery and asserts backend.main + person_detector + the segmenter registry import
  cleanly — the LIGHT-tier guarantee, enforced.
- test_person_detector.py: the detect_and_track test injected its mock via the module-level
  `person_detector.torch` attribute (now gone); inject it through sys.modules instead so the
  in-method `import torch` binds to the mock.

Full suite 1332 passed / 7 skipped, ruff + mypy clean. Verified the no-torch import works; the HEAVY
person-detector path is unchanged.

### 1047. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.502Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Open PR for P2e
command: cd C:/Users/avidu/Projects/bhi-wt-p2e && gh pr create --base master --head feat/packaging-p2e-lazy-torch --title "feat(pkg): P2e — lazy-torch so the LIGHT tier imports with NO torch installed" --body "$(cat <<'BODY'
## What & why
**PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2e.** The plan's **LIGHT tier** (Gemini / motion / cpu-mock) ships **no torch** — but `import backend.main` actually pulled torch at module load via the *one* eager top-level `import torch` in `person_detector.py`. So the "no torch" promise didn't hold. This defers that import, making the LIGHT tier genuinely torch-free (smaller install, faster startup).

## Changes
- **`person_detector.py`** — drop the top-level `import torch`; import it **lazily inside the two methods that use it** (`lazy_load_model`, `detect_and_track`), alongside the already-lazy torchvision/supervision imports. `cv2` stays top-level (opencv-python is a hard dep, always installed). The person cue is **HEAVY-only** (yolo/fusion) and never instantiated under LIGHT, so this is pure deferral — no behaviour change on the HEAVY path (torch present there).
- **`tests/test_light_tier_no_torch.py`** *(new)* — spawns a fresh interpreter with `torch`/`torchvision` **blocked at the import machinery** and asserts `backend.main` + `person_detector` + the segmenter registry import cleanly. The LIGHT-tier guarantee, enforced in CI.
- **`test_person_detector.py`** — the `detect_and_track` test injected its mock via the module-level `person_detector.torch` attribute (now gone); inject through `sys.modules` so the in-method `import torch` binds to the mock.

## Verification (local)
- The blocked-torch subprocess test passes: `backend.main` imports with torch absent.
- Full suite **1332 passed / 7 skipped**, `ruff` + `mypy` clean.
- The HEAVY person-detector path is unchanged (its tests pass with the lazy import).

This makes the **LIGHT-tier install** real: a user can `pip install` the engine + run `indexer --app` on Gemini/motion **without** the multi-hundred-MB torch download.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
BODY
)" 2>&1 | tail -3
